9:T54a,### 1. 62nd RBI Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) Meeting (August 2026)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep | **Revision Effort:** ~10 min | **Category:** RBI & Monetary Policy
* **Source:** CGB Mentors & Smartkeeda | **Event Date:** August 3–5, 2026

**What Happened**
* The 6-member Monetary Policy Committee (MPC), chaired by **Governor Sanjay Malhotra**, kept the Policy Repo Rate unchanged at **5.25%**.
* **External Members**: Dr. Nagesh Kumar, Saugata Bhattacharya, Prof. Ram Singh.
* **Internal Members**: Sanjay Malhotra (Governor), Dr. Poonam Gupta, Indranil Bhattacharyya.

**Must Memorize (Core Exam Numbers)**
* **Policy Repo Rate**: `5.25%` (Unchanged).
* **Real GDP Growth (FY 2026–27)**: Projected at **6.7%** ($Q_1$: 7.0%, $Q_2$: 6.4%, $Q_3$: 6.5%, $Q_4$: 6.8%).
* **CPI Inflation (FY 2026–27)**: Lowered to **5.0%** (earlier 5.1%) ($Q_2$: 4.7%, $Q_3$: 5.9%, $Q_4$: 5.5%).
* **Core Inflation (FY27)**: Projected at **4.3%**.
* **External Stability**: Q1 Gross FDI at **$30.7 billion**; Forex reserves cover **$>10$ months** of imports; External debt cover at **90.8%**.

**Know / Understand**
* Core inflation is anchored at 4.3%, but food supply risks (El Niño disruptions affecting pulses and vegetables) prevent premature monetary easing.
* ⚠️ **Change-Sensitive:** Next MPC meeting scheduled for October 5–7, 2026.

---a:T7bf,### 1. RBI Draft Master Directions: Interest Rates on Loans and Advances 2026
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep | **Revision Effort:** ~8 min | **Category:** Banking Regulation & Lending
* **Status:** **DRAFT / CONSULTATION** | **Proposed Effective Date:** **1 April 2027**
* **Verification Status:** Source-only (CGB Mentors p.6–7; primary-source verification pending)

**What Happened**
* The RBI issued draft directions titled *RBI (Interest Rates on Loans and Advances) Directions, 2026* inviting stakeholder comments.

**Must Memorize (Draft Proposals)**
* **Benchmark Pricing**: Lenders can offer loans at fixed or floating rates linked to an internal or external benchmark plus a risk-based spread. A lender **would not be allowed to price a loan below the applicable benchmark**.
* **Reset Frequency for Floating Loans**:
  * The benchmark, reset frequency, and reset date must be clearly specified in the loan agreement.
  * The reset frequency is chosen by the lender, but **cannot be more than once every 3 months**.
  * Once fixed for a loan, the reset frequency **would remain unchanged through the loan tenor** (subject to specified exemptions for smaller cooperative banks, certain NBFCs, and some UCBs).
* **Agricultural Loans**: Reset period linked to the crop season, but **cannot exceed 12 months**.
* **Internal Benchmark (MCLR)**: Commercial banks, RRBs, Tier 3 & Tier 4 urban cooperative banks, and rural cooperative banks with deposits $>₹1,000\text{ crore}$ would continue to use an internal benchmark based on the marginal cost of funds, referenced as **MCLR**.
* **External Benchmark (EBLR)**: Retains mandatory external benchmark linkage for all floating-rate personal loans and floating-rate loans to MSMEs extended by commercial banks (for RRBs, co-ops, NBFCs, and AIFIs, external benchmark linkage is not mandatory).
* **Interest Application**: The draft proposes that interest on advances should generally be charged at **monthly rests**.

---b:Tdde,### 1. RBI Issues Reserve Bank of India (Internal Ombudsman) Directions, 2026
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-01-26
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued the comprehensive **Reserve Bank of India (Internal Ombudsman) Directions, 2026**, overhauling and strengthening the internal grievance redressal architecture across regulated entities.
* The Directions supersede and repeal the erstwhile *Master Direction - Reserve Bank of India (Internal Ombudsman for Regulated Entities) Directions, 2023* (dated December 29, 2023).
* The new framework comes into full effect from **June 30, 2026**.
* **Applicability:** Covers six categories of regulated entities: (1) Commercial Banks, (2) Small Finance Banks (SFBs), (3) Payments Banks, (4) Non-Banking Financial Companies (NBFCs), (5) Non-Bank Prepaid Payment Instrument (PPI) Issuers, and (6) Credit Information Companies (CICs).

#### Must Memorize
* **Appointment Criteria for Internal Ombudsman (IO):** Must be a retired or serving officer in the rank equivalent to a **General Manager (GM)** in a regulated entity under the IO framework or a financial sector regulatory body, with a minimum of **7 years of relevant experience**.
* **Appointment Criteria for Deputy IO:** Must be a retired or serving officer in the rank equivalent to a **Deputy General Manager (DGM)** with a minimum of **5 years of relevant experience**.
* **Age Cap:** The IO / Deputy IO must not be over **70 years of age** before the completion of tenure.
* **Tenure:** Fixed term of **not less than 3 years**, and total tenure (including extensions/reappointments) **shall not exceed 5 years**.
* **Vacancies:** In case of an unexpected vacancy, the bank must inform the RBI within **10 working days** and appoint a replacement.
* **Automated Escalation Rule:** Regulated entities must deploy a fully automated Complaints Management System where all complaints **partially resolved or wholly rejected** by the bank's internal grievance redressal mechanism are **auto-escalated to the IO** for independent review.
* **Review Timelines:**
  * For complaints governed by RBI, NPCI, or card network resolution deadlines: IO gets at least **10 days** for review.
  * In all other cases: review within **20 days** of receipt.
  * Final decision communication to complainant: within a maximum of **30 days** from initial receipt of complaint by the bank.
* **Compensation Powers under RBI Ombudsman Scheme:**
  * No monetary limit on the dispute amount brought before the RBI Ombudsman for settlement or award.
  * Consequential loss compensation: **up to ₹30 lakh**.
  * Compensation for harassment, mental anguish, time lost, and expenses: **up to ₹3 lakh**.

#### Know / Understand
* The Internal Ombudsman is an independent, internal dispute-resolution officer sitting between the bank's front-line grievance desk and the external RBI Ombudsman.
* Auto-escalation prevents frontline bank staff from arbitrarily closing rejected consumer complaints without independent internal oversight.

#### Exam Focus
* Exact eligibility rank (GM for IO, DGM for Dy IO) and experience thresholds (7 years vs 5 years).
* Age ceiling (70 years) and tenure constraints (min 3 years, max 5 years).
* Auto-escalation trigger: partial resolution or rejection by bank's internal team.
* Compensation ceilings: ₹30 lakh (consequential loss) and ₹3 lakh (mental anguish/harassment).

---c:Tb42,### 2. RBI Tightens Related Party Transactions & Credit Risk Norms for Banks and NBFCs
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-01-15
* **Revision Effort:** ~7 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued amendments to its *Credit Risk Management Directions* for commercial banks and non-banking financial companies (NBFCs), establishing a stringent, harmonized oversight framework for Related Party Transactions (RPTs).
* The amended rules take effect from **April 1, 2026**.
* The reform aligns the definition of "Related Party" with the **Companies Act, 2013** and the **Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (IBC)**, bringing NBFC regulations closer to commercial bank standards.

#### Must Memorize
* **Definition of Related Party:** Promoters, directors, Key Managerial Personnel (KMP) and their relatives, shareholders holding **10% or more equity**, entities where such persons exercise significant influence/control, and trust structures where related persons are trustees, authors, or beneficiaries.
* **Reciprocally Related Persons (Banks only):** Extends coverage to directors of other banks/FIs, trustees of Alternative Investment Funds (AIFs) and Mutual Funds (MFs), and their relatives.
* **Scope of Lending:** Includes both fund-based and non-fund-based credit facilities, as well as investments in debt instruments of related parties. **Explicitly excludes equity investments** from the definition of lending.
* **Transaction-Level Materiality Approval Thresholds:**
  * **Banks with total assets > ₹10 lakh crore:** Related-party loan exceeding **₹50 crore** requires Board / Board Committee approval.
  * **Banks with total assets ₹1 lakh crore – ₹10 lakh crore:** Threshold is **₹10 crore**.
  * **Banks with total assets < ₹1 lakh crore:** Threshold is **₹5 crore**.
  * **Top Layer / Upper Layer NBFCs:** Threshold is **₹10 crore**.
  * **Middle Layer NBFCs:** Threshold is **₹5 crore**.
  * **Base Layer NBFCs:** Threshold is **₹1 crore**.
* **Prohibitions:** Explicit prohibition on credit exposures to promoters and their relatives, or entities where shareholders holding 10%+ equity exercise significant control.

#### Know / Understand
* Prevents siphoning of bank and NBFC depositor funds to promoter-controlled entities and connected shell companies.
* Asset tiering ensures proportional regulatory burden: Mega-banks (>₹10T assets) have higher rupee thresholds (₹50 cr) to avoid board-level gridlock on small transactions.

#### Exam Focus
* Materiality threshold matrix across bank asset sizes (₹50 cr, ₹10 cr, ₹5 cr) and NBFC layers (₹10 cr, ₹5 cr, ₹1 cr).
* Treatment of equity investments (excluded from lending definition).
* Shareholder threshold for related-party tagging (10% or more equity).

---d:T906,### 3. MoSPI Overhauls Macroeconomic Series: CPI Base Year Revised to 2023-24
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** MACRO_ECONOMY
* **Institution:** OTHER
* **Event Date:** 2026-01-12
* **Revision Effort:** ~7 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Ministry of Statistics and Programme Implementation (MoSPI) announced the complete modernization of India's macroeconomic statistical architecture, updating the base years of Consumer Price Index (CPI), Gross Domestic Product (GDP), and Index of Industrial Production (IIP).
* The final release of the old CPI series (Base 2012=100) took place for December 2025 data (1.33% provisional inflation).
* The new CPI series with **Base Year 2023-24=100** takes effect on **February 12, 2026**.

#### Must Memorize
* **New Base Years & Rollout Schedule:**
  * **CPI Series:** Base Year **2023-24=100**, released on **12 February 2026** (first used for January 2026 inflation).
  * **GDP Series:** Base Year **2022-23=100**, released on **27 February 2026**.
  * **IIP Series:** Base Year **2022-23=100**, released on **28 May 2026**.
* **CPI Weightage Structural Shift:**
  * **Food & Beverages Weight:** Reduced sharply from **45.86% to 36.75%** (a 9.11 percentage point drop).
  * **Housing Weight (incl. water, electricity, gas):** Increased from **10.07% to 17.66%**.
  * **Item Basket Expansion:** Increased from **299 items to 358 items**.
* **Historical Context:** India's last CPI base year update was in January 2015 (setting 2011-12 as base, replacing 2004-05).

#### Know / Understand
* Engel's Law in Action: As Indian household incomes rose between 2011-12 and 2023-24, the proportion of monthly expenditure on food declined, while spending on housing, transport, and modern services surged.
* Macro impact: A lower food weight significantly reduces the volatility of headline CPI inflation caused by seasonal vegetable and crop shocks, allowing RBI monetary policy to reflect core economic demand more accurately.

#### Exam Focus
* Exact dates for new series releases (CPI: Feb 12, GDP: Feb 27, IIP: May 28, 2026).
* New base years (CPI 2023-24; GDP & IIP 2022-23).
* Food weight reduction (45.86% $\to$ 36.75%) and housing weight expansion (10.07% $\to$ 17.66%).
* Total item basket expansion (299 $\to$ 358 items).

---e:T79f,### 4. RBI Eases Risk Weights on NBFC Infrastructure Project Lending
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Memorize
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-01-20
* **Revision Effort:** ~4 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued *Amendment Directions* easing its proposed prudential risk-weight framework for infrastructure project lending by Non-Banking Financial Companies (NBFCs).
* Broadened the eligibility criteria for "high-quality" infrastructure projects and relaxed the debt repayment thresholds required to qualify for lower risk weights.
* **Effective Date:** April 1, 2026, with full adoption required by the next exposure review or **March 31, 2027**, whichever is earlier.

#### Must Memorize
* **Relaxed Debt Repayment Thresholds:**
  * Threshold to assign a **75% risk weight:** Reduced to **2% repayment** of original sanctioned project debt (cut from the earlier proposed 5%).
  * Threshold to assign a **50% risk weight:** Reduced to **5% repayment** of original sanctioned project debt (cut from the earlier proposed 10%).
* **Expanded "High-Quality" Definition:** Includes infrastructure projects where revenues arise from concessions or contracts granted by the Central Government, State Governments, Public Sector Enterprises, or statutory/regulatory bodies.
* **Measurement Standard:** Repayment is calculated against the original sanctioned project debt (with additional debt clubbed for calculation).

#### Know / Understand
* Infrastructure loans are long-gestation assets with high initial default risk during construction. Lowering the repayment thresholds (2% and 5%) allows NBFCs to release regulatory capital earlier as projects operationalize.

#### Exam Focus
* New repayment thresholds for risk weight concessions: 2% (for 75% RW) and 5% (for 50% RW).
* Effective date (April 1, 2026) and final transition deadline (March 31, 2027).

---f:Tb64,### 1. DICGC Notifies Risk-Based Premium (RBP) Framework for Deposit Insurance
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-02-08
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Deposit Insurance and Credit Guarantee Corporation (DICGC), with the approval of the Reserve Bank of India (RBI), advised insured banks on the implementation of the **Risk-Based Premium (RBP) framework**.
* Replaces the flat-rate premium system of 12 paise per ₹100 of assessable deposits that has operated since 1962, exercising powers under **Section 15(1) of the DICGC Act, 1961**.
* **Effective Implementation Date:** **April 1, 2026**, with mandatory review at least once every three years.

#### Must Memorize
* **Two Risk Assessment Models:**
  * **Tier 1 Model:** Applicable to Scheduled Commercial Banks (SCBs) other than Regional Rural Banks (RRBs). Evaluated on supervisory ratings, quantitative CAMELS parameters, and potential loss to the Deposit Insurance Fund (DIF) in case of failure.
  * **Tier 2 Model:** Applicable to RRBs and Co-operative Banks. Evaluated on quantitative CAMELS parameters and potential loss to DIF.
* **Incentive Limits & Formula:**
  * **Maximum Risk Model Incentive:** Up to **33.33%** over the card rate.
  * **Maximum Vintage Model Incentive:** Up to **25%** for prolonged contribution to the DIF without major distress or claim payouts.
  * **Effective Premium Calculation Formula:**
    $$\text{Effective Rate} = \text{Card Rate} \times (1 - \text{Risk\_model\_incentive}) \times (1 - \text{Vintage\_model\_incentive})$$
* **Exemptions & Flat Card Rate Retentions:**
  * **Local Area Banks (LABs) and Payments Banks (PBs):** Continue paying the flat card rate of **12 paise per ₹100 of assessable deposits per annum** (due to data limitations; they represent $<1\%$ of total premium collected).
  * **UCBs under SAF / PCA:** Banks under the Supervisory Action Framework (SAF) or Prompt Corrective Action (PCA) continue paying the flat card rate of 12 paise; eligible for RBP from the financial year following exit from SAF/PCA.
* **Confidentiality Mandate:** Banks are legally required to maintain strict confidentiality of supervisory ratings and are prohibited from disclosing their RBP rating or premium amount paid to DICGC.

#### Know / Understand
* Moves India's deposit insurance pricing from a mutualized subsidy model (where sound banks subsidize risky banks) to a risk-sensitive prudential discipline aligning bank insurance cost with asset risk and governance health.

#### Exam Focus
* Legal section: Section 15(1) of DICGC Act, 1961.
* Effective date: April 1, 2026 (reviewed every 3 years).
* Maximum incentive caps: 33.33% (Risk Model) and 25% (Vintage Model).
* Entities retaining flat 12 paise card rate: Payments Banks, Local Area Banks, and UCBs under SAF/PCA.

---10:T7c8,### 2. RBI Revises MSME Lending Norms: Collateral-Free Limit Raised to ₹20 Lakh
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-02-11
* **Revision Effort:** ~7 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued the **Lending to Micro, Small & Medium Enterprises (MSME) Sector (Amendment) Directions, 2026**, revising collateral requirements under the master directions.
* Comes into full effect from **April 1, 2026**.
* Enhances the mandatory collateral-free lending ceiling for micro and small enterprises to ease credit flow without asset encumbrance.

#### Must Memorize
* **Mandatory Collateral-Free Limit:** Banks are mandated **not to accept collateral security** for loans **up to ₹20 lakh** extended to units in the Micro and Small Enterprise (MSE) sector.
* **PMEGP Linkage:** Mandatory collateral-free threshold of **₹20 lakh** applies to all units financed under the **Prime Minister's Employment Generation Programme (PMEGP)** administered by KVIC.
* **Discretionary Enhancement:** Banks may, based on financial position and track record, increase the collateral-free threshold **up to ₹25 lakh** as per board-approved internal policy.
* **Voluntary Gold/Silver Pledges:** Accepting gold and silver voluntarily pledged by borrowers for loans up to the collateral-free limit is explicitly clarified as **not a violation** of the mandate.
* **Credit Guarantee Scheme:** Banks may avail the benefit of Credit Guarantee Scheme cover (e.g. CGTMSE) where applicable.

#### Know / Understand
* Eliminates collateral bottlenecks for early-stage micro-enterprises and artisan units while maintaining lender risk protection via credit guarantee mechanisms.

#### Exam Focus
* Exact mandatory collateral-free limit (₹20 lakh).
* Discretionary upper limit for good track-record units (₹25 lakh).
* Application to PMEGP units (₹20 lakh).
* Effective date: April 1, 2026.

---11:T80d,### 3. RBI Notifies Amended ECB Framework: Borrowing Limit Raised to $1 Billion
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-02-15
* **Revision Effort:** ~7 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India notified the **Foreign Exchange Management (Borrowing and Lending) (First Amendment) Regulations, 2026**, overhauling the External Commercial Borrowing (ECB) framework.
* Liberalizes borrowing limits, rationalizes minimum average maturity, eliminates the all-in-cost monitoring by AD banks, and recognizes corporate control acquisition as an eligible end-use.

#### Must Memorize
* **Revised Borrowing Limit:** Replaced the erstwhile $750 million cap with **the higher of $1 billion or 300% of the borrower's latest audited standalone net worth** per financial year under the automatic route.
* **Maturity Requirements:** Minimum average maturity of **3 years** for general ECBs; manufacturing sector companies may raise up to **$150 million** with shorter maturities between **1 and 3 years**.
* **End-Use Liberalization:** Acquisition of corporate **"control"** is officially confirmed as a permitted end-use. On-lending of ECB funds to individuals is permitted for RBI-regulated entities (on-lending for real estate business remains prohibited).
* **Operational Streamlining:** Removed the mandatory requirement for designated Authorized Dealer (AD) banks to maintain a current account or verify alignment with prevailing market borrowing costs following the removal of all-in-cost ceilings.

#### Know / Understand
* Gives Indian corporates and infrastructure entities greater flexibility to access global capital pools and execute cross-border mergers and acquisitions without restrictive borrowing caps.

#### Exam Focus
* New automatic route limit: Higher of $1 billion or 300% of standalone net worth.
* Minimum average maturity: 3 years (1–3 years for manufacturing up to $150M).
* Treatment of acquisition of control (permitted end-use).

---12:T57c,### 4. 16th Finance Commission Submits Devolution Report for 2026–2031 (41% Retained)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Memorize
* **Category:** MACRO_ECONOMY
* **Institution:** MINISTRY_OF_FINANCE
* **Event Date:** 2026-02-01
* **Revision Effort:** ~4 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The **16th Finance Commission** report for tax revenue devolution between the Centre and States for the 5-year award period **2026–2031** was formally laid in the Lok Sabha.
* The Commission was constituted on **December 31, 2023** under **Article 280 of the Indian Constitution**, chaired by **Dr. Arvind Panagariya** (former Vice Chairman of NITI Aayog).

#### Must Memorize
* **Tax Devolution Share:** Recommended retaining the **41% vertical share** of central divisible tax pool devolution to States (maintaining the allocation in place since 2021 following the reorganization of Jammu & Kashmir).
* **Award Period:** 5 years covering **FY 2026-27 to FY 2030-31**.
* **Constitutional Anchor:** Article 280 of the Constitution of India.

#### Know / Understand
* Balances fiscal stability of the Union government with the resource demands of States for capital expenditure and social infrastructure.

#### Exam Focus
* 16th Finance Commission Chairman: Dr. Arvind Panagariya.
* Recommended tax devolution percentage: 41%.
* Award period: 2026–2031.
* Constitutional Article: Article 280.

---13:Tbd7,### 1. Insolvency & Bankruptcy Code (Amendment) Bill, 2025: Introduction of CIIRP & Structural Timelines
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** MINISTRY_OF_FINANCE
* **Event Date:** 2026-03-12
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Parliament passed the landmark **Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code (Amendment) Bill, 2025**, introducing 12 comprehensive structural amendments (11 recommended by the Select Committee and 1 by the Government) to eliminate procedural bottlenecks, improve creditor recovery, and streamline corporate resolution.
* Introduces a completely new resolution framework titled **Creditor-Initiated Insolvency Resolution Process (CIIRP)** under **Chapter IV-A** of the Code.

#### Must Memorize
* **Creditor-Initiated Insolvency Resolution Process (CIIRP — Chapter IV-A):**
  * Financial creditors holding **at least 51% of voting share** can directly initiate insolvency.
  * Compressed resolution timeline: **150 days** (with a single maximum extension of **45 days**).
  * Debtor-in-Possession Model: Management remains with existing promoters but is continuously supervised by the Resolution Professional (RP).
* **Strict Admission & Approval Timelines:**
  * Application admission or rejection by NCLT (under Sections 7, 9, and 10): strictly within **14 days** (mandatory recording of reasons for delay).
  * Resolution plan approval by NCLT: strictly within **30 days**.
* **Revival of Failed CIRP:**
  * CIRP can be revived **once before liquidation** within **120 days** of failure.
* **Supervision of Liquidation:**
  * Liquidation process must be completed within **180 days** (maximum extension of **90 days**).
  * Committee of Creditors (CoC) retains supervisory powers during liquidation and can replace the liquidator with a **66% voting majority**. Secured creditors must exercise rights within **14 days** or lose priority.
* **Withdrawal of Insolvency Applications:**
  * Permitted **only after CoC constitution** and **before the invitation of resolution plans**, requiring **90% approval of the CoC**.
* **Dissenting Financial Creditor Rights:**
  * Dissenting financial creditors must receive at least the **minimum liquidation value** or proportionate distribution.
* **Penalties:** Frivolous filings attract penalties of **₹1 lakh to ₹2 crore**; non-compliance penalties up to **₹5 crore** or **3× the gains/losses**.

#### Know / Understand
* CIIRP provides an out-of-court, debtor-in-possession alternative to CIRP, preventing business disruption while giving financial creditors direct resolution powers without waiting for management default paralysis.

#### Exam Focus
* Voting threshold to initiate CIIRP (51%) and replace liquidator (66%).
* CIIRP timeline (150 + 45 days) vs Liquidation timeline (180 + 90 days).
* Mandatory admission timeline for NCLT (14 days) and resolution plan approval (30 days).
* CoC threshold to approve withdrawal of insolvency application (90%).

---14:Ta4c,### 2. Government Notifies Income Tax Rules, 2026 (Under Income-tax Act, 2025)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** MACRO_ECONOMY
* **Institution:** MINISTRY_OF_FINANCE
* **Event Date:** 2026-03-10
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Ministry of Finance notified the **Income Tax Rules, 2026**, operationalizing the new **Income-tax Act, 2025** (which supersedes the six-decade-old Income-tax Act, 1961) with effect from **April 1, 2026**.
* Substantially condenses and simplifies direct tax architecture: Sections reduced from **819 to 536**, Chapters from **47 to 23**, and total word count cut from 5.12 lakh words to 2.6 lakh words.

#### Must Memorize
* **Expansion of 50% HRA Metro Exemption:**
  * Expanded from 4 metro cities to **8 major urban centres**: **Mumbai, Kolkata, Delhi, Chennai, Hyderabad, Pune, Ahmedabad, and Bengaluru** receive 50% of basic salary HRA exemption. All other cities receive 40%.
* **Mandatory PAN Quoting Thresholds:**
  * **Cash Deposits & Withdrawals:** Mandatory for aggregate cash transactions of **₹10 lakh or more in a financial year** (replacing the old single-day ₹50,000 threshold).
  * **Motor Vehicles / Two-Wheelers:** Mandatory for purchase exceeding **₹5 lakh**.
  * **Hotel / Restaurant / Event Banquets:** Mandatory for payments exceeding **₹1 lakh** (earlier ₹50,000).
  * **Immovable Property (Purchase/Sale/Gift/JDA):** Mandatory for property value exceeding **₹20 lakh** (earlier ₹10 lakh).
* **Credit Card & Cross-Border Surveillance:**
  * Mandatory linking of PAN with credit cards (no credit card can be issued without PAN). Annual spending above **₹10 lakh** reported to tax authorities.
* **Corporate & Market Compliance:**
  * Stock exchanges must maintain electronic audit trails for **7 years**.
  * Zero-Coupon Bond Framework: Requires application 3 months prior to issuance and investment-grade ratings from at least **two credit rating agencies**.
  * Significant Economic Presence (SEP) for digital/remote businesses: Threshold set at **₹2 crore transaction value or 3 lakh users**.

#### Know / Understand
* Moves India's direct tax framework into the digital era by aligning HRA exemptions with modern economic hubs and replacing easily circumvented daily cash limits with annual PAN-linked reporting.

#### Exam Focus
* 8 cities eligible for 50% HRA exemption.
* New annual PAN cash deposit/withdrawal threshold (₹10 lakh/year).
* PAN threshold for property transactions (₹20 lakh) and motor vehicles (₹5 lakh).
* Stock exchange audit trail retention requirement (7 years).

---15:T957,### 3. RBI Responsible Business Conduct Directions 2026: Online Fraud Compensation & 2FA Mandate
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-03-08
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued the **Reserve Bank of India (Commercial Banks – Responsible Business Conduct) Third Amendment Directions, 2026**, coming into effect from **July 1, 2026**.
* Overhauls customer protection, limits customer liability in unauthorized digital transactions, and mandates dynamic Two-Factor Authentication (2FA).

#### Must Memorize
* **Compensation for Small-Value Online Frauds:**
  * Customers reporting a gross loss of up to **₹50,000** due to fraudulent electronic banking transactions receive **85% of the net loss amount or ₹25,000, whichever is less**, once during their lifetime.
  * Eligibility: Loss must be proven genuine under bank policy and reported within **5 days** to both the bank and the National Cyber Crime Reporting Portal / Cyber Helpline (**1930**).
* **Zero Liability Criteria:**
  * Customer receives full zero liability and immediate reversal if fraud occurs due to bank deficiency/negligence, or in third-party breaches reported to the bank within **5 calendar days** of occurrence.
* **Mandatory Two-Factor Authentication (2FA):**
  * Mandatory 2FA for all domestic digital transactions (UPI, cards, wallets) from **April 1, 2026**, requiring at least one **dynamic factor** (e.g. OTP, biometric).
  * Deadline for cross-border card-not-present transactions: **October 1, 2026**.
* **Mandatory Instant SMS Alerts:**
  * Banks must mandatorily send instant SMS alerts for all electronic banking transactions **exceeding ₹500** (optional for $\le ₹500$ as per bank policy).

#### Know / Understand
* Provides an institutional safety net for retail depositors against small cyber thefts while obligating banks to invest in real-time fraud monitoring systems like MuleHunter.AI.

#### Exam Focus
* Small-value fraud compensation cap (85% of net loss or ₹25,000, whichever is lower; once in lifetime).
* Maximum gross loss eligible for small fraud relief (₹50,000) and reporting window (5 days; Helpline 1930).
* 2FA implementation date (April 1, 2026; Cross-border Oct 1, 2026).
* Mandatory SMS alert threshold (>₹500).

---16:T736,### 4. RBI Master Guidelines on Bank Dividend Declaration & Adjusted PAT Formula
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Memorize
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-03-05
* **Revision Effort:** ~5 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India finalized and notified its amended *Directions on Declaration of Dividends by Banks*, establishing a capital-linked distribution regime taking effect from **FY 2026-27 (FY27)**.
* Replaced the erstwhile static 40% dividend cap with a risk-sensitive payout ratio of up to **75% of Profit After Tax (PAT)**.

#### Must Memorize
* **Maximum Dividend Cap:** Commercial banks incorporated in India can declare dividends up to **75% of Profit After Tax (PAT)** for the relevant financial year.
* **Capital Buffer Matrix:** Payout capacity is determined by a bank's Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital position:
  * Banks with CET1 ratios only marginally above regulatory minimums are **prohibited from declaring any dividend**.
  * Banks with significantly higher capital positions may distribute up to **100% of Adjusted PAT**, subject to the overall statutory ceiling of **75% of actual PAT**.
* **Adjusted PAT Formula:**
  $$\text{Adjusted PAT} = \text{PAT of Financial Year} - 50\% \text{ of Net Non-Performing Assets (Net NPAs as of March 31)}$$
* **Effective Date:** Applies to all dividend declarations starting from **FY 2026-27 (FY27)**.

#### Know / Understand
* Prevents under-capitalized banks from eroding their solvency through aggressive dividend payouts, while allowing well-capitalized, low-NPA lenders greater freedom to reward shareholders.

#### Exam Focus
* Maximum statutory dividend payout cap (75% of PAT).
* Adjusted PAT deduction formula (deduction of 50% of Net NPAs).
* Effective fiscal year (FY 2026–27).

---17:Ta88,### 1. RBI Finalizes Expected Credit Loss (ECL) Provisioning Norms (Effective April 1, 2027)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-04-12
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India finalized and issued comprehensive guidelines for commercial banks to transition from the incurred-loss provisioning model to the forward-looking **Expected Credit Loss (ECL) provisioning framework**.
* **Implementation Date:** Takes effect from **April 1, 2027**.
* Mandates banks to estimate and reserve funds for potential credit losses before default occurs, establishing a 4-year transition glidepath to cushion the capital impact.

#### Must Memorize
* **Three-Stage Asset Classification & Provisioning Floor:**
  * **Stage 1 (Normal / Performing Loans):** 12-month expected credit loss; minimum provisioning floor of **0.4%** for standard corporate and retail advances.
  * **Stage 2 (Underperforming / Significant Increase in Credit Risk - SICR):** Lifetime expected credit loss (e.g., 60–90 days overdue); minimum provisioning floor of **5%**.
  * **Stage 3 (Impaired / Non-Performing Assets - NPAs):** Full lifetime loss provision for loans with objective default evidence (90+ DPD).
* **Four-Year Capital Transition Glidepath:**
  * Banks can spread the incremental provisioning requirement over **four years ending March 31, 2031**.
  * Transitional Capital Relief: Banks are permitted to add back the net tax-adjusted provision increase to **Common Equity Tier 1 (CET1) capital** during the transition window ending March 31, 2031.
* **Three Key Computational Parameters:**
  * **Probability of Default (PD):** Likelihood of borrower defaulting over 12 months or lifetime.
  * **Loss Given Default (LGD):** Percentage of exposure lost if default occurs.
  * **Exposure at Default (EAD):** Total gross value exposed to default risk.
* **Effective Interest Rate (EIR) Mandate:** All loans outstanding as of March 31, 2027 must transition to the Effective Interest Rate regime no later than **March 31, 2030**.

#### Know / Understand
* The incurred-loss model recognized provisions only after a loan became an NPA (lagging indicator). ECL forces banks to provision dynamically as macroeconomic or credit conditions deteriorate, preventing sudden balance sheet shocks.

#### Exam Focus
* 3 stages of ECL classification (Stage 1: 12-month loss / 0.4%, Stage 2: Lifetime loss / 5%, Stage 3: Full lifetime loss).
* 4-year transition window and CET1 add-back deadline (March 31, 2031).
* 3 core parameters: PD, LGD, EAD.
* EIR transition deadline: March 31, 2030.

---18:T875,### 2. RBI Finalizes Basel III Standardised Credit Risk Capital Directions
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-04-14
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued final directions on Basel III Capital Charges for Credit Risk under the Standardised Approach, establishing refined risk weights for unrated corporate debt, bank exposures, and retail lending.
* **Effective Date:** Implemented from **April 1, 2027**.

#### Must Memorize
* **Unrated Corporate & NBFC Exposures:**
  * The threshold for applying a punitive **150% risk weight** on unrated corporate and NBFC exposures was raised to **₹500 crore** (relaxed from the ₹200 crore threshold proposed earlier).
* **Unrated Bank Exposures:**
  * Long-term unrated bank exposures carry a **100% risk weight**.
  * Short-term unrated bank exposures carry a **50% risk weight**. Foreign bank branches in India are permitted to use parent bank ratings.
* **Regulatory Retail Exposure Framework:**
  * Scope expanded to include all small businesses (not restricted to MSMEs) with annual turnover up to **₹500 crore**.
  * Individual borrower regulatory retail exposure cap increased from ₹7.5 crore to **₹10 crore**.
* **Commercial Real Estate (CRE) & ADC Projects:**
  * Minimum borrower equity contribution for Acquisition, Development, and Construction (ADC) projects set at **25% of total project cost (including land)**.
  * Upward property revaluation for additional loans permitted only after a minimum of **5 years** from loan origination or possession.

#### Know / Understand
* Raising the unrated punitive risk weight threshold from ₹200 cr to ₹500 cr prevents credit contraction for mid-sized Indian enterprises that rely on bank credit without formal bond ratings.

#### Exam Focus
* Unrated 150% risk weight threshold: ₹500 crore.
* Regulatory retail exposure ceiling: ₹10 crore (turnover up to ₹500 crore).
* Unrated bank exposure risk weights: 100% (long-term) and 50% (short-term).
* ADC project minimum equity requirement: 25%.

---19:T685,### 3. 60th RBI Monetary Policy Committee (MPC) Statement (April 2026)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** MONETARY_POLICY
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-04-08
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The 6-member Monetary Policy Committee (MPC), chaired by **Governor Sanjay Malhotra**, conducted its 60th meeting from April 6 to 8, 2026, keeping all key policy rates unchanged and maintaining a **Neutral stance**.
* **MPC Composition:** Internal Members: Sanjay Malhotra (Governor), Dr. Poonam Gupta, Indranil Bhattacharyya; External Members: Dr. Nagesh Kumar, Saugata Bhattacharya, Prof. Ram Singh.

#### Must Memorize
* **Policy Rates Matrix:**
  * **Policy Repo Rate:** **5.25%** (Unchanged).
  * **Standing Deposit Facility (SDF) Rate:** **5.00%**.
  * **Marginal Standing Facility (MSF) Rate:** **5.50%**.
  * **Bank Rate:** **5.50%**.
  * **Monetary Policy Stance:** **Neutral**.
* **Growth & Inflation Forecasts for FY 2026-27 (FY27):**
  * **Real GDP Growth:** Projected at **6.9%** (Q1: 6.8%, Q2: 6.7%, Q3: 7.0%, Q4: 7.2%).
  * **CPI Inflation:** Projected at **4.6%** (Q1: 4.0%, Q2: 4.4%, Q3: 5.2%, Q4: 4.7%).
  * **Core Inflation:** Projected at **4.4%** for FY27.
* **Meeting Calendar:** Next MPC meeting scheduled for **June 3 to 5, 2026**.

#### Know / Understand
* Inflation projection lowered to 4.6% as supply-side pressures ease and the new CPI base year (2023-24) takes full statistical effect.

#### Exam Focus
* Exact Policy Repo Rate (5.25%), SDF (5.00%), MSF/Bank Rate (5.50%).
* FY27 GDP projection (6.9%) and CPI inflation projection (4.6%).
* Policy stance (Neutral).

---1a:T8d5,### 4. RBI Cancels Banking License of Paytm Payments Bank Limited under Section 22(4)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Memorize
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-04-24
* **Revision Effort:** ~5 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India cancelled the banking license of **Paytm Payments Bank Limited (PPBL)** under **Section 22(4) of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949**, with effect from **April 24, 2026**.
* Followed persistent non-compliance with Section 22(3)(b) (affairs conducted detrimental to depositors), Section 22(3)(c) (character of management prejudicial to public interest), and Section 22(3)(e).
* Prohibits PPBL from accepting deposits or conducting any banking business under Section 5(b) or Section 6 of the BR Act, 1949.

#### Must Memorize
* **Legal Grounds:** Cancelled under Section 22(4) read with Section 22(3) of Banking Regulation Act, 1949.
* **Depositor Protection:** RBI confirmed PPBL maintains sufficient liquidity to repay 100% of deposit liabilities upon winding up.
* **Regulatory Profile of Payments Banks:**
  * Recommended by the **Nachiket Mor Committee**.
  * Statutory Requirements: Licensed under Section 22 of BR Act 1949; minimum paid-up equity capital of **₹100 crore**; maximum deposit limit of **₹2,00,000 per individual customer**.
  * First Payments Bank: **Airtel Payments Bank** (launched 2017). First Govt-Owned: **India Post Payments Bank (IPPB)** (launched Sept 1, 2018).
  * **Active Operating Payments Banks in India (5 Entities):** (1) Airtel Payments Bank, (2) India Post Payments Bank, (3) Fino Payments Bank, (4) Jio Payments Bank, (5) NSDL Payments Bank.

#### Know / Understand
* Payments banks are restricted, non-lending deposit entities designed for remittances and financial inclusion. License cancellation enforces zero tolerance for anti-money laundering (AML) and KYC supervisory violations.

#### Exam Focus
* Statutory Section used for cancellation (Section 22(4) of BR Act 1949).
* Nachiket Mor Committee recommendations for Payments Banks.
* Payments Bank statutory capital (₹100 crore) and maximum deposit ceiling (₹2 lakh).
* Total count of operating Payments Banks remaining in India (5 banks).

---1b:T8ab,### 1. 623rd RBI Central Board Meeting: Record ₹2.86 Lakh Crore Dividend & CRB at 6.5%
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-05-22
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The 623rd meeting of the Central Board of Directors of the Reserve Bank of India was held in Mumbai under the chairmanship of **Governor Sanjay Malhotra**.
* Approved a historic surplus transfer (dividend payout) of **₹2,86,588.46 crore** to the Central Government for the accounting year 2025-26, surpassing the previous record of ₹2.68 lakh crore.
* Maintained the **Contingent Risk Buffer (CRB)** at **6.5%** of the RBI balance sheet within the Economic Capital Framework (ECF) target band of 4.5% to 7.5%.

#### Must Memorize
* **Surplus Dividend Transfer:** Approved **₹2,86,588.46 crore** transfer to the Government of India for FY 2025-26 (highest-ever dividend payout by the central bank).
* **Contingent Risk Buffer (CRB) Allocation:** Transferred **₹1,09,379.64 crore** to the CRB (compared to ₹44,861.70 crore in FY25) to maintain the buffer at **6.5%** of the balance sheet.
* **Balance Sheet Expansion:** RBI's balance sheet expanded by **20.61% to ₹91,97,121.08 crore** as of March 31, 2026.
* **Financial Performance:**
  * Gross income increased by **26.42%**.
  * Net income (before risk provisions and statutory fund transfers) reached **₹3,95,972.10 crore**.
* **Economic Capital Framework (ECF) Parameters:** Flexibility to maintain CRB within the range of **4.5% to 7.5%** of total assets.

#### Know / Understand
* Robust earnings from higher interest on domestic and foreign securities, coupled with massive spot forex interventions ($53.13 billion net sales), enabled the central bank to fortify its risk buffers while delivering critical fiscal support to the Union Budget.

#### Exam Focus
* Exact surplus dividend amount transferred to the Government (₹2,86,588.46 crore).
* CRB percentage maintained (6.5%) and ECF prescribed range (4.5%–7.5%).
* Amount transferred to CRB (₹1,09,379.64 crore).
* Total size of the RBI balance sheet as of March 31, 2026 (₹91.97 lakh crore).

---1c:T90a,### 2. RBI Finalizes Revised Lending & Housing Loan Norms for Urban Co-operative Banks (UCBs)
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-05-15
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India issued final directions on revised lending norms for Urban Co-operative Banks (UCBs), establishing aggregate and individual caps on unsecured advances and tightening tenure/moratorium limits on housing loans.
* **Effective Date:** Guidelines take effect from **October 1, 2026**.

#### Must Memorize
* **Aggregate & Individual Unsecured Lending Ceilings:**
  * **Aggregate Ceiling:** Unsecured loans and advances capped at **20% of total loans and advances** (shifting from the earlier limit of 10% of total assets).
  * **Tier-Specific Individual Ceilings:**
    * **Tier-1 UCBs:** Maximum **₹5 lakh** per borrower.
    * **Tier-2 UCBs:** Maximum **₹7.5 lakh** per borrower.
    * **Tier-3 & Tier-4 UCBs:** Maximum **₹10 lakh** per borrower.
  * **Priority Sector PSL Exemption:** Unsecured advances up to **₹50,000 per borrower** qualifying as PSL are excluded from the 20% aggregate cap for UCBs meeting RBI's Eligibility Criteria for Business Authorisation (ECBA).
* **Housing Loan Regulations for UCBs:**
  * **Tenure Cap:** Maximum housing loan tenure capped at **20 years** (inclusive of moratorium) for Tier-1 and Tier-2 UCBs. (Tier-3 and Tier-4 UCBs determine tenures under board-approved policies).
  * **Moratorium Restrictions:** Moratorium permitted **only for under-construction properties**, capped at a maximum of **24 months** (moratoriums prohibited for ready-to-move-in housing purchases).

#### Know / Understand
* Restricts unsecured exposure risks in smaller cooperative lenders while ensuring credit flow to micro-borrowers and preventing speculative maturity mismatches in urban housing finance.

#### Exam Focus
* Aggregate unsecured lending cap (20% of total loans).
* Individual unsecured caps across UCB Tiers: Tier-1 (₹5L), Tier-2 (₹7.5L), Tier-3/4 (₹10L).
* Small PSL loan exclusion threshold (₹50,000).
* Housing loan tenure cap for Tier-1/2 UCBs (20 years) and maximum construction moratorium (24 months).
* Effective date: October 1, 2026.

---1d:T83c,### 3. RBI Overhauls Foreign Exchange Authorization & Forex Correspondent (FxC) Model
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Deep
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-05-12
* **Revision Effort:** ~8 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India overhauled the authorization and renewal framework for entities dealing in foreign exchange under the **Foreign Exchange Management (Authorized Persons) Regulations, 2026**.
* Introduced the **Forex Correspondent (FxC)** model allowing AD Category-I and AD Category-II entities to appoint agents for retail foreign exchange operations.

#### Must Memorize
* **Forex Correspondent (FxC) Operating Framework:**
  * Authorized Dealers (AD-I and AD-II) can appoint FxCs under a principal-agent model.
  * Permitted FxC Operations: (1) Purchase foreign currency notes/coins and travellers' cheques; (2) Sell foreign currency notes/travellers' cheques for foreign travel; (3) Act as MTSS sub-agents.
* **Transaction Limits & Net Worth Requirements:**
  * **AD Category-II Transaction Cap:** Capped at **₹25 lakh per foreign trade transaction**.
  * **Minimum Net Worth:**
    * **AD Category-II:** **₹10 crore**.
    * **AD Category-III:** **₹2 crore**.
  * **Minimum Annual Turnover Requirement:** Must achieve minimum annual turnover of **₹50 crore (AD Category-II)** and **₹10 crore (FFMCs)** within two years of operation and maintain it continuously.
* **Jurisdictional Permissions:** AD Category-I banks retain full access to all current and capital account transactions.

#### Know / Understand
* Expands retail foreign exchange availability and digital remittance points across non-metro regions while enforcing institutional accountability through parent AD bank balance sheets.

#### Exam Focus
* Eligible parent entities to appoint FxCs (AD-I and AD-II).
* AD Category-II transaction ceiling (₹25 lakh per trade transaction).
* Minimum net worth: ₹10 crore (AD-II) and ₹2 crore (AD-III).
* Minimum 2-year turnover thresholds: ₹50 crore (AD-II) and ₹10 crore (FFMC).

---1e:T6e9,### 4. RBI Discontinues Mandatory Investment Fluctuation Reserve (IFR) for Banks
* **Priority:** P1 — Critical / Memorize
* **Category:** BANKING_REGULATION
* **Institution:** RBI
* **Event Date:** 2026-05-18
* **Revision Effort:** ~5 min
* **Status:** NOTIFIED

#### What Happened
* The Reserve Bank of India discontinued the mandatory requirement for commercial banks to create and maintain an **Investment Fluctuation Reserve (IFR)** as an additional buffer against investment portfolio depreciation.
* **Effective Date:** Came into force on **May 18, 2026**.

#### Must Memorize
* **Discontinuation & Balance Transfer Protocol:**
  * Requirement for commercial banks to maintain IFR stands discontinued from May 18, 2026.
  * Existing IFR balances as of May 17, 2026 shall be transferred **'below the line' to Statutory Reserve, General Reserve, or the Balance of Profit and Loss Account**.
* **Foreign Bank Branches in India:**
  * Balance in IFR transferred directly to statutory reserves kept in Indian books or non-repatriable remittable surplus retained in Indian books.
* **Small Finance Banks (SFBs) & Payments Banks (PBs):**
  * Clarified that transfers to IFR must be made from net profit after mandatory statutory appropriations.

#### Know / Understand
* IFR was introduced to absorb yield fluctuations in Available for Sale (AFS) and Held for Trading (HFT) portfolios. With the implementation of revised investment classification norms and mark-to-market accounting under Basel III, standalone IFR had become redundant.

#### Exam Focus
* Effective date of IFR discontinuation: May 18, 2026.
* Accounting treatment of existing IFR balances (transferred 'below the line' to Statutory/General Reserve or P&L).
* Treatment for foreign bank branches.

voting majority**.
